82: // newTestNetns creates a named network namespace without leaving the
83: // current thread in it.
84: func newTestNetns(t *testing.T) (string, *netlink.Handle) {
85: 	t.Helper()
86: 
87: 	if os.Geteuid() != 0 {
88: 		t.Skip("requires root to create network namespaces")
89: 	}
90: 
91: 	runtime.LockOSThread()
92: 	defer runtime.UnlockOSThread()
93: 
94: 	orig, err := netns.Get()
95: 	if err != nil {
96: 		t.Fatalf("failed to get current netns: %v", err)
97: 	}
98: 	defer orig.Close()
99: 
100: 	name := fmt.Sprintf("clabtst-ev-%d-%d", os.Getpid(), time.Now().UnixNano())
101: 
102: 	ns, err := netns.NewNamed(name)
103: 	if err != nil {
104: 		t.Fatalf("failed to create netns: %v", err)
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	if err := netns.Set(orig); err != nil {
108: 		t.Fatalf("failed to return to the original netns: %v", err)
109: 	}
110: 
111: 	handle, err := netlink.NewHandleAt(ns)
112: 	if err != nil {
113: 		t.Fatalf("failed to create netlink handle: %v", err)
114: 	}
115: 
116: 	t.Cleanup(func() {
117: 		handle.Close()
118: 		ns.Close()
119: 		_ = netns.DeleteNamed(name)
120: 	})
121: 
122: 	return "/run/netns/" + name, handle
123: }
124:

129: 	if err != nil {
130: 		t.Errorf("failed to add %s: %v", name, err)
131: 	}
132: }

143: 	).AnyTimes()
144:

147: 		ShortID: "0123456789ab",

155: func startRegistry(
156: 	t *testing.T,
157: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
158: ) (*netlinkRegistry, *eventLog) {
159: 	t.Helper()
160: 
161: 	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
162: 	events := make(chan aggregatedEvent, 1024)
163: 	registry := newNetlinkRegistry(ctx, events, true, false, 0)
164: 	collected := collectEvents(ctx, events)
165: 
166: 	registry.Start(container)
167: 	t.Cleanup(func() {
168: 		registry.Stop(container.ID)
169: 		cancel()
170: 	})
171: 
172: 	return registry, collected
173: }

175: // TestNetlinkWatcherReportsExistingAndNewLinks checks that links present at
176: // subscription are reported as snapshots and later ones as created or deleted.
177: func TestNetlinkWatcherReportsExistingAndNewLinks(t *testing.T) {
178: 	nsPath, handle := newTestNetns(t)
179: 	addDummy(t, handle, "early0")
180: 
181: 	container := newTestContainer(t, func() (string, error) { return nsPath, nil })
182: 	_, events := startRegistry(t, container)
183: 
184: 	events.waitFor(t, []string{"lo", "early0"}, "snapshot")
185: 
186: 	addDummy(t, handle, "late0")
187: 	events.waitFor(t, []string{"late0"}, "create")
188: 
189: 	link, err := handle.LinkByName("late0")
190: 	if err != nil {
191: 		t.Fatalf("failed to find late0: %v", err)
192: 	}
193: 
194: 	if err := handle.LinkDel(link); err != nil {
195: 		t.Fatalf("failed to delete late0: %v", err)
196: 	}
197: 
198: 	events.waitFor(t, []string{"late0"}, "delete")
199: 
200: 	if got := events.get("early0"); slices.Contains(got, "create") {
201: 		t.Fatalf("early0 was reported as created: %v", got)
202: 	}
203: }

269: 	watchMaxAttempts, watchRetryDelay = 2, 10*time.Millisecond
270: 	t.Cleanup(func() { watchMaxAttempts, watchRetryDelay = origAttempts, origDelay })

286: // TestProcessExistingAfterResubscribe checks how a listed link is reported on
287: // the first subscription and after a resubscribe.
288: func TestProcessExistingAfterResubscribe(t *testing.T) {
289: 	link := func(mtu int) netlink.Link {
290: 		return &netlink.Dummy{LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{Index: 7, Name: "eth1", MTU: mtu}}
291: 	}
292: 
293: 	tests := map[string]struct {
294: 		includeSnapshot bool
295: 		initial         bool
296: 		known           netlink.Link
297: 		vanished        bool
298: 		want            []string
299: 	}{
300: 		"first subscription": {
301: 			includeSnapshot: true,
302: 			initial:         true,
303: 			want:            []string{"snapshot"},
304: 		},
305: 		"first subscription without snapshot": {initial: true},
306: 		"new after resubscribe":               {want: []string{"create"}},
307: 		"changed after resubscribe":           {known: link(1400), want: []string{"update"}},
308: 		"unchanged after resubscribe":         {known: link(1500)},
309: 		"deleted while resubscribing":         {vanished: true},
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	for name, tt := range tests {
313: 		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
314: 			events := make(chan aggregatedEvent, 8)
315: 			registry := newNetlinkRegistry(t.Context(), events, tt.includeSnapshot, false, 0)
316: 			watcher := &netlinkWatcher{
317: 				container:       &clabruntime.GenericContainer{Names: []string{"n1"}},
318: 				includeSnapshot: tt.includeSnapshot,
319: 			}
320: 
321: 			states := map[int]ifaceSnapshot{}
322: 			if tt.known != nil {
323: 				states[7] = snapshotFromLink(tt.known)
324: 			}
325: 
326: 			var vanished map[int]struct{}
327: 			if tt.vanished {
328: 				vanished = map[int]struct{}{7: {}}
329: 			}
330: 
331: 			watcher.processExisting(states, nil, link(1500), nil, vanished, registry, tt.initial)
332: 			close(events)
333: 
334: 			var got []string
335: 			for ev := range events {
336: 				got = append(got, ev.Action)
337: 			}
338: 
339: 			if !slices.Equal(got, tt.want) {
340: 				t.Fatalf("actions = %v, want %v", got, tt.want)
341: 			}
342: 
343: 			if tt.vanished {
344: 				if _, ok := states[7]; ok {
345: 					t.Fatal("vanished link is tracked again")
346: 				}
347: 
348: 				return
349: 			}
350: 
351: 			if states[7].MTU != 1500 {
352: 				t.Fatalf("state MTU = %d, want 1500", states[7].MTU)
353: 			}
354: 		})
355: 	}
356: }

358: // TestProcessUpdateDeleteOnlyTrackedLinks checks that a deletion is reported
359: // once, also when its notification arrives after a resubscribe has already
360: // reported it.
361: func TestProcessUpdateDeleteOnlyTrackedLinks(t *testing.T) {
362: 	link := &netlink.Dummy{LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{Index: 7, Name: "eth1", MTU: 1500}}
363: 	update := netlink.LinkUpdate{Link: link}
364: 	update.Header.Type = unix.RTM_DELLINK
365: 
366: 	events := make(chan aggregatedEvent, 8)
367: 	registry := newNetlinkRegistry(t.Context(), events, false, false, 0)
368: 	watcher := &netlinkWatcher{
369: 		container: &clabruntime.GenericContainer{Names: []string{"n1"}},
370: 	}
371: 
372: 	states := map[int]ifaceSnapshot{7: snapshotFromLink(link)}
373: 
374: 	watcher.processUpdate(states, nil, update, registry, nil)
375: 	watcher.processUpdate(states, nil, update, registry, nil)
376: 	close(events)
377:

379: 	for ev := range events {
380: 		got = append(got, ev.Action)
381: 	}
382:

384: 		t.Fatalf("actions = %v, want %v", got, want)
385: 	}
386: 
387: 	if _, ok := states[7]; ok {
388: 		t.Fatal("deleted link is still tracked")
389: 	}
390: }
391: 
392: // reportedEvent is the action and interface name of an emitted event.
393: type reportedEvent struct {
394: 	action string
395: 	ifname string
396: }
397: 
398: // drainEvents closes events and returns what was emitted, in order.
399: func drainEvents(events chan aggregatedEvent) []reportedEvent {
400: 	close(events)
401: 
402: 	var got []reportedEvent
403: 	for ev := range events {
404: 		got = append(got, reportedEvent{action: ev.Action, ifname: ev.Attributes["ifname"]})
405: 	}
406: 
407: 	return got
408: }
409: 
410: // stitchedWatcher returns a watcher that reported the link with index 7 as
411: // the host-side stitch interface of its veth-stitch link.
412: func stitchedWatcher() *netlinkWatcher {
413: 	return &netlinkWatcher{
414: 		container: &clabruntime.GenericContainer{Names: []string{"n1"}},
415: 		reported: map[int]reportedInterface{
416: 			7: {
417: 				attributes: map[string]string{"ifname": "clab-s-0011aabb", "alias": "e1-1"},
418: 				since:      time.Now(),
419: 			},
420: 		},
421: 	}
422: }

425: // name than before is first reported deleted under the old name, so clients

aggregatedEvent) *eventLog {\n\tl := &eventLog{actions: map[string][]string{}}\n\n\tgo func() {\n\t\tfor {\n\t\t\tselect {\n\t\t\tcase ev := <-events:\n\t\t\t\tl.mu.Lock()\n\t\t\t\tname :=  []string\n\t}{\n\t\t\"first subscription\": {\n\t\t\tincludeSnapshot: true,\n\t\t\tinitial:         true,\n\t\t\twant:            []string{\"snapshot\"},\n\t\t},\n\t\t\"first subscription without 459: // processExisting records a link listed when the subscription started. On the

466: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processExisting(

527:

539:

541: }
542: 
543: func (w *netlinkWatcher) processUpdate(
544: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
545: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
546: 	update netlink.LinkUpdate,
547: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
548: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
549: ) {
550: 	if update.Link == nil {
551: 		return
552: 	}
553:

555: 	if attrs == nil {
556: 		return
557: 	}
558:

560: 	previous, exists := states[snapshot.Index]
561: 
562: 	switch update.Header.Type {
563: 	case unix.RTM_DELLINK:
564: 		// A link that is not tracked was never reported, or its deletion was
565: 		// already reported when the links were listed again after a resubscribe.
566: 		if !exists {
567: 			return
568: 		}
569: 
570: 		delete(states, snapshot.Index)
571: 		delete(statsSamples, snapshot.Index)
572: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", previous)
573: 	case unix.RTM_NEWLINK:
574: 		// Query netem info for this interface
575: 		snapshot = withNetem(snapshot, netemQuery.Query())
576: 
577: 		if exists && snapshot.equal(previous) {
578: 			return
579: 		}
580: 
581: 		action := "create"
582: 		if exists {
583: 			action = "update"
584: 		}
585:

587: 		if w.includeStats && statsSamples != nil {
588: 			if sample, ok := newStatsSample(snapshot, time.Now()); ok {
589: 				statsSamples[snapshot.Index] = sample
590: 			}
591: 		}
592: 		registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, action, snapshot)
593: 	}
594: }
595: 
596: // pollNetemChanges checks for netem changes and emits update events.
597: func (w *netlinkWatcher) pollNetemChanges(
598: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
599: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
600: 	netemQuery *netemQuerier,
601: ) {
602: 	netemInfos := netemQuery.Query()

604: 	for idx, snapshot := range states {
605: 		previous := snapshot
606: 		info, hasNetem := netemInfos[idx]
607: 
608: 		if hasNetem {
609: 			snapshot.HasNetem = true
610: 			snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
611: 			snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
612: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
613: 			snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
614: 			snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
615: 		} else if snapshot.HasNetem {
616: 			// Netem was removed
617: 			snapshot.HasNetem = false
618: 			snapshot.Delay = ""
619: 			snapshot.Jitter = ""
620: 			snapshot.PacketLoss = 0
621: 			snapshot.Rate = 0
622: 			snapshot.Corruption = 0
623: 		}
624: 
625: 		if !snapshot.equal(previous) {
626: 			states[idx] = snapshot
627: 			registry.emitInterfaceEvent(w.container, "update", snapshot)
628: 		}
629: 	}

Such a link is skipped; its\n// deletion notification follows and is ignored, as the link is not tracked.\nfunc (w *netlinkWatcher) processExisting(\n\tstates map[int]ifaceSnapshot,\n\tstatsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,\n\tlink netlink.Link,\n\tnetemInfos map[int]netemInfo,\n\tvanished map[int]struct{},\n\tregistry *netlinkRegistry,\n\tinitial bool,\n) {\n\tif link == nil || link.Attrs() == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif _, ok := vanished[link.Attrs().Index]; 812: func (r *netlinkRegistry) emitInterfaceEvent(
813: 	container *clabruntime.GenericContainer,
814: 	action string,
815: 	snapshot ifaceSnapshot,
816: ) {
817: 	if container == nil {
818: 		return
819: 	}

822:

824: 		Timestamp:   time.Now(),

836: 	}

839: func waitForNamespacePath(
840: 	ctx context.Context,
841: 	runtime clabruntime.ContainerRuntime,
842: 	containerID string,
843: ) (string, error) {
844: 	const (
845: 		attempts   = 5
846: 		retryDelay = 200 * time.Millisecond
847: 	)
848: 
849: 	var lastErr error
850: 
851: 	for i := 0; i < attempts; i++ {
852: 		nsPath, err := runtime.GetNSPath(ctx, containerID)
853: 		if err == nil && nsPath != "" {
854: 			return nsPath, nil
855: 		}
856: 
857: 		if err != nil {
858: 			lastErr = err
859: 		}
860: 
861: 		select {
862: 		case <-time.After(retryDelay):
863: 		case <-ctx.Done():
864: 			return "", ctx.Err()
865: 		}
866: 	}
867: 
868: 	if lastErr != nil {
869: 		return "", lastErr
870: 	}
871: 
872: 	return "", fmt.Errorf("namespace path not ready for container %s", containerID)
873: }

903: func snapshotInterfaces(netHandle *netlink.Handle) (map[int]ifaceSnapshot, error) {
904: 	if netHandle == nil {
905: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netlink handle is nil")
906: 	}
907: 
908: 	links, err := netHandle.LinkList()
909: 	if err != nil {
910: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to list links: %w", err)
911: 	}
912: 
913: 	states := make(map[int]ifaceSnapshot, len(links))
914: 	for _, link := range links {
915: 		snapshot := snapshotFromLink(link)
916: 		states[snapshot.Index] = snapshot
917: 	}
918: 
919: 	return states, nil
920: }

922: // withNetem returns the snapshot with the netem settings of its interface.
923: func withNetem(snapshot ifaceSnapshot, netemInfos map[int]netemInfo) ifaceSnapshot {
924: 	if info, ok := netemInfos[snapshot.Index]; ok {
925: 		snapshot.HasNetem = true
926: 		snapshot.Delay = info.Delay
927: 		snapshot.Jitter = info.Jitter
928: 		snapshot.PacketLoss = info.PacketLoss
929: 		snapshot.Rate = info.Rate
930: 		snapshot.Corruption = info.Corruption
931: 	}
932: 
933: 	return snapshot
934: }

936: func snapshotFromLink(link netlink.Link) ifaceSnapshot {
937: 	attrs := link.Attrs()
938: 
939: 	snapshot := ifaceSnapshot{
940: 		Type: link.Type(),
941: 	}
942: 
943: 	if attrs != nil {
944: 		snapshot.Index = attrs.Index
945: 		snapshot.Name = attrs.Name
946: 		snapshot.Alias = attrs.Alias
947: 		snapshot.MTU = attrs.MTU
948: 		if len(attrs.HardwareAddr) > 0 {
949: 			snapshot.MAC = attrs.HardwareAddr.String()
950: 		}
951: 		snapshot.OperState = attrs.OperState.String()
952: 		if stats := attrs.Statistics; stats != nil {
953: 			snapshot.HasStats = true
954: 			snapshot.RxBytes = stats.RxBytes
955: 			snapshot.TxBytes = stats.TxBytes
956: 			snapshot.RxPackets = stats.RxPackets
957: 			snapshot.TxPackets = stats.TxPackets
958: 		}
959: 	}
960: 
961: 	return snapshot
962: }

996: 		s.Jitter == other.Jitter &&

1023: func newStatsSample(snapshot ifaceSnapshot, timestamp time.Time) (ifaceStatsSample, bool) {
1024: 	if !snapshot.HasStats {
1025: 		return ifaceStatsSample{}, false
1026: 	}
1027: 
1028: 	return ifaceStatsSample{
1029: 		RxBytes:   snapshot.RxBytes,
1030: 		TxBytes:   snapshot.TxBytes,
1031: 		RxPackets: snapshot.RxPackets,
1032: 		TxPackets: snapshot.TxPackets,
1033: 		Timestamp: timestamp,
1034: 	}, true

1037: func (w *netlinkWatcher) collectAndEmitStats(
1038: 	netHandle *netlink.Handle,
1039: 	states map[int]ifaceSnapshot,
1040: 	statsSamples map[int]ifaceStatsSample,
1041: 	registry *netlinkRegistry,
1042: ) {
1043: 	if netHandle == nil || statsSamples == nil {
1044: 		return
1045: 	}
1046: 
1047: 	now := time.Now()
1048: 	// Fetch all interfaces with one netlink call per tick instead of one call
1049: 	// per interface to reduce syscall overhead in large labs.
1050: 	currentStates, err := snapshotInterfaces(netHandle)
1051: 	if err != nil {
1052: 		log.Debugf(
1053: 			"failed to collect interface stats snapshot for container %s: %v",
1054: 			firstContainerName(w.container),
1055: 			err,
1056: 		)
1057: 
1058: 		return
1059: 	}
1060: 
1061: 	for idx, state := range states {
1062: 		current, ok := currentStates[idx]
1063: 		if !ok {
1064: 			continue
1065: 		}
1066: 
1067: 		state.Name = current.Name
1068: 		state.Alias = current.Alias
1069: 		state.MTU = current.MTU
1070: 		state.MAC = current.MAC
1071: 		state.OperState = current.OperState
1072: 		state.Type = current.Type
1073: 		state.HasStats = current.HasStats
1074: 		state.RxBytes = current.RxBytes
1075: 		state.TxBytes = current.TxBytes
1076: 		state.RxPackets = current.RxPackets
1077: 		state.TxPackets = current.TxPackets
1078: 		states[idx] = state

1081: 			delete(statsSamples, idx)
1082:

1084: 		}

1087: 		if !ok || now.Sub(prev.Timestamp) <= 0 {

1089: 				RxBytes:   state.RxBytes,
1090: 				TxBytes:   state.TxBytes,
1091: 				RxPackets: state.RxPackets,
1092: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,

1097: 		}

1105: 				TxPackets: state.TxPackets,
1106: 				Timestamp: now,
1107: 			}
1108: 
1109: 			continue
1110: 		}

1112: 		rxBytesDelta := deltaCounter(prev.RxBytes, state.RxBytes)
1113: 		txBytesDelta := deltaCounter(prev.TxBytes, state.TxBytes)
1114: 		rxPacketsDelta := deltaCounter(prev.RxPackets, state.RxPackets)
1115: 		txPacketsDelta := deltaCounter(prev.TxPackets, state.TxPackets)

1155: func deltaCounter(previous, current uint64) uint64 {
1156: 	if current >= previous {
1157: 		return current - previous
1158: 	}

1160: 	return current

1187: // Query queries the TC qdiscs in the namespace and returns a map of
1188: // interface index to netem data.
1189: func (q *netemQuerier) Query() map[int]netemInfo {
1190: 	result := make(map[int]netemInfo)
1191: 
1192: 	qdiscs, err := q.qdiscs()
1193: 	if err != nil {
1194: 		log.Debugf("failed to query tc qdiscs: %v", err)
1195: 		return result
1196: 	}
1197: 
1198: 	for idx := range qdiscs {

1201: 			continue
1202: 		}
1203: 
1204: 		info := netemInfoFromQdisc(qdisc)
1205: 		if info.hasValues() {
1206: 			result[int(qdisc.Ifindex)] = info
1207: 		}
1208: 	}
1209: 
1210: 	return result
1211: }
1212: 
1213: func (q *netemQuerier) qdiscs() ([]gotc.Object, error) {
1214: 	if q == nil {
1215: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem querier is nil")
1216: 	}
1217: 
1218: 	if err := q.ensureHandle(); err != nil {
1219: 		return nil, err
1220: 	}
1221: 
1222: 	qdiscs, err := clabnetem.Impairments(q.tcHandle)
1223: 	if err == nil {
1224: 		return qdiscs, nil
1225: 	}
1226: 
1227: 	// Retry once by reopening the tc socket in case it became stale.
1228: 	q.Close()
1229: 	if errOpen := q.ensureHandle(); errOpen != nil {
1230: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
1231: 			"failed to query tc qdiscs (%v), and failed to reopen tc socket (%v)",
1232: 			err,
1233: 			errOpen,
1234: 		)
1235: 	}
1236: 
1237: 	return clabnetem.Impairments(q.tcHandle)
1238: }
1239: 
1240: func (q *netemQuerier) ensureHandle() error {
1241: 	if q == nil {
1242: 		return fmt.Errorf("netem querier is nil")
1243: 	}
1244: 
1245: 	if q.tcHandle != nil {
1246: 		return nil
1247: 	}

1250: 	if err != nil {
1251: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open tc socket for netem query: %w", err)
1252: 	}
1253: 
1254: 	q.tcHandle = tcnl
1255: 
1256: 	return nil
1257: }

1271: func netemInfoFromQdisc(qdisc *gotc.Object) netemInfo {
1272: 	var info netemInfo
1273: 
1274: 	if qdisc.Netem == nil {
1275: 		return info
1276: 	}
1277: 
1278: 	if qdisc.Netem.Latency64 != nil && *qdisc.Netem.Latency64 != 0 {
1279: 		info.Delay = (time.Duration(*qdisc.Netem.Latency64) * time.Nanosecond).String()
1280: 	}
1281: 
1282: 	if qdisc.Netem.Jitter64 != nil && *qdisc.Netem.Jitter64 != 0 {
1283: 		info.Jitter = (time.Duration(*qdisc.Netem.Jitter64) * time.Nanosecond).String()
1284: 	}
1285: 
1286: 	if qdisc.Netem.Rate != nil && qdisc.Netem.Rate.Rate != 0 {
1287: 		info.Rate = int(qdisc.Netem.Rate.Rate * 8 / msPerSec)
1288: 	}
1289: 
1290: 	if qdisc.Netem.Corrupt != nil && qdisc.Netem.Corrupt.Probability != 0 {
1291: 		info.Corruption = math.Round((float64(qdisc.Netem.Corrupt.Probability)/
1292: 			float64(math.MaxUint32)*100)*100) / 100
1293: 	}
1294: 
1295: 	if qdisc.Netem.Qopt.Loss != 0 {
1296: 		info.PacketLoss = math.Round(
1297: 			(float64(qdisc.Netem.Qopt.Loss)/float64(math.MaxUint32)*100)*100) / 100
1298: 	}
1299: 
1300: 	return info

71: // LinkVEthStitched is the resolved veth-stitch link: two veth segments
72: // (nodeA<->root-ns, nodeB<->root-ns) whose root-ns ends are joined by a tc stitch.

74: 	LinkCommonParams
75:

77:

103: // Deploy creates the veth segment belonging to ep.
104: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) Deploy(ctx context.Context, ep Endpoint) error {
105: 	l.lifecycleMutex.Lock()
106: 	defer l.lifecycleMutex.Unlock()
107: 
108: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
109: 		return nil
110: 	}
111: 
112: 	var err error
113: 	switch ep {
114: 	case l.segA.Endpoints[0]:
115: 		err = l.segA.Deploy(ctx, ep)
116: 	case l.segB.Endpoints[0]:
117: 		err = l.segB.Deploy(ctx, ep)
118: 	default:
119: 		return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %s does not belong to link [ %s, %s ]",
120: 			ep, l.segA.Endpoints[0], l.segB.Endpoints[0])
121: 	}
122: 	if err != nil {
123: 		return err
124: 	}
125: 
126: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed
127: 
128: 	if l.segA.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed &&
129: 		l.segB.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
130: 		return l.stitchSegments(ctx)
131: 	}
132: 
133: 	return nil
134: }
135: 
136: // PostDeploy tags the two root-ns far ends and joins them with tc.
137: // if they weren't already stitched in Deploy.
138: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context) error {
139: 	l.lifecycleMutex.Lock()
140: 	defer l.lifecycleMutex.Unlock()
141: 
142: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
143: 		return nil
144: 	}
145: 	if l.segA.DeploymentState != LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed ||
146: 		l.segB.DeploymentState != LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
147: 		return fmt.Errorf("cannot stitch veth segments before both are fully deployed")
148: 	}
149: 
150: 	return l.stitchSegments(ctx)
151: }
152: 
153: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) stitchSegments(ctx context.Context) error {
154: 	steps := []func() error{
155: 		func() error { return markFarEnd(l.epB, l.segA.Endpoints[0], l.labName) },
156: 		func() error { return markFarEnd(l.epA, l.segB.Endpoints[0], l.labName) },
157: 		func() error { return stitch(l.epA, l.epB) },
158: 		func() error { return stitch(l.epB, l.epA) },
159: 	}
160: 	for _, step := range steps {
161: 		if err := retryTransientNetlink(ctx, "veth-stitch", step); err != nil {
162: 			return err
163: 		}
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed
167: 
168: 	return nil
169: }
170: 
171: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
172: 	l.lifecycleMutex.Lock()
173: 	defer l.lifecycleMutex.Unlock()
174: 
175: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
176: 		return nil
177: 	}
178: 
179: 	err := errors.Join(l.segA.Remove(ctx), l.segB.Remove(ctx))
180: 
181: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateRemoved
182: 
183: 	return err
184: }
185: 
186: // buildVEthStitchSegment builds one veth segment: the real node endpoint plus a
187: // root-namespace far end named with a hash.
188: func buildVEthStitchSegment(
189: 	params *ResolveParams,
190: 	owner *LinkVEthStitched,
191: 	nodeEpRaw *EndpointRaw,
192: 	mtu int,
193: ) (*LinkVEth, Endpoint, error) {

196: 
197: 	nodeEp, err := nodeEpRaw.Resolve(params, owner)
198: 	if err != nil {
199: 		return nil, nil, err
200: 	}
201: 
202: 	// the far end sits in the root namespace (host node) with a short hash name
203: 	// that fits the 15 char name limit.
204: 	farName := stitchFarEndName(params.LabName, nodeEpRaw.Node, nodeEpRaw.Iface)
205: 	farGeneric := NewEndpointGeneric(GetHostLinkNode(), farName, seg)
206: 	farGeneric.MAC = nodeEp.GetMac()
207: 	farEp := NewEndpointHost(farGeneric)
208: 	seg.Endpoints = []Endpoint{nodeEp, farEp}
209: 
210: 	return seg, farEp, nil
211: }
212: 
213: func stitchFarEndName(labName, node, iface string) string {
214: 	sum := sha1.Sum([]byte(labName + "\x00" + node + "\x00" + iface))
215: 	return "clab-s-" + hex.EncodeToString(sum[:4]) // 7 + 8 = 15 chars
216: }
217: 
218: func markFarEnd(farEp, nodeEp Endpoint, labName string) error {
219: 	l, err := netlink.LinkByName(farEp.GetIfaceName())
220: 	if err != nil {
221: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to look up veth-stitch far end %q: %w", farEp.GetIfaceName(), err)
222: 	}
223:

225: 	if iface == "" {
226: 		iface = nodeEp.GetIfaceName()
227: 	}
228: 
229: 	altName := StitchAltName(labName, nodeEp.GetNode().GetShortName(), iface)
230: 	if err := netlink.LinkAddAltName(l, altName); err != nil && !isAltNameNotSupportedErr(err) {
231: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to add veth-stitch altname %q: %w", altName, err)
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	return nil
235: }

237: func validateVEthStitched(r *LinkVEthStitchedRaw) error {

239: 		return fmt.Errorf(

241: 			len(r.Endpoints),
242: 		)
243: 	}
244:

246: }

249: 	ctx context.Context,

252: ) error {

256: func cleanupFilteredVethStitch(
257: 	ctx context.Context,
258: 	labName string,
259: 	nodesFilter []string,
260: 	endpoints []*EndpointRaw,
261: ) error {
262: 	for _, altName := range filteredVethStitchAltNames(labName, nodesFilter, endpoints) {
263: 		if err := RemoveOwnedInterface(ctx, GetHostLinkNode(), altName); err != nil {
264: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed cleaning up veth-stitch interface %q: %w", altName, err)
265: 		}
266: 	}
267:

269: }
270: 
271: func filteredVethStitchAltNames(
272: 	labName string,
273: 	nodesFilter []string,
274: 	endpoints []*EndpointRaw,
275: ) []string {
276: 	names := make(map[string]struct{})
277: 	for _, ep := range endpoints {
278: 		if ep == nil || ep.Node == "" || ep.Iface == "" ||
279: 			!slices.Contains(nodesFilter, ep.Node) {
280: 			continue
281: 		}
282: 		names[StitchAltName(labName, ep.Node, ep.Iface)] = struct{}{}
283: 	}
284: 
285: 	result := make([]string, 0, len(names))
286: 	for name := range names {
287: 		result = append(result, name)
288: 	}
289: 	sort.Strings(result)
290: 
291: 	return result
292: }
293: 
294: // StitchAltName returns a unique name for veth-stitch links.
295: func StitchAltName(labName, node, iface string) string {
296: 	return "clab-stitch-" + clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(labName+"-"+node+"-"+iface)
297: }
298: 
299: // ToolsInterface returns the host-netns interface veth-stitch (and similar
300: // kinds) publish for node:iface under StitchAltName, or (nil, false) if none.
301: // Single seam for reporting/impairing the host side of the stitch.
302: func ToolsInterface(labName, node, iface string) (netlink.Link, bool) {
303: 	if labName == "" || node == "" || iface == "" {
304: 		return nil, false
305: 	}
306: 
307: 	link, err := netlink.LinkByName(StitchAltName(labName, node, iface))
308: 	if err != nil {
309: 		return nil, false
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	return link, true
313: }

interface veth-stitch (and similar\n// kinds) publish for node:iface under StitchAltName, or (nil, false) if none.\n// Single seam for reporting/impairing the host side of the stitch.\nfunc 1. New `reported` map on watcher: tracks what each link was last reported as (attributes + since).
2. `emit` replaces `emitInterfaceEvent` calls: tracks reported state, converts `update`→`create` when the link was reported under a different ifname before (after sending a delete under the old name).
3. `emitStats` moves stats emission to watcher; also emits a create event if the link was renamed (track returns true).
4. `reportStitchMarks`: on netem ticker tick, for veth links reported under their own name within stitchMarkWindow (1 minute), check whether the host-side stitch interface now exists (via `interfaceAttributes` which calls `overlayToolsInterface` → `ToolsInterface` → `netlink.LinkByName` in root namespace). If ifname differs (i.e., stitch interface now exists), emit an update (which will trigger track → delete old name → create new name... wait).

Wait — in `reportStitchMarks`, it calls `w.emit(registry, "update", snapshot)`. Inside emit, attributes = interfaceAttributes(...) which now resolves to stitch name. `track` sees previous ifname == snapshot.Name (the node's own name e.g. "eth1"), and new attributes ifname is "clab-s-..." — different → sends delete under previous name, returns true → action "update" → becomes "create". Then sends create with new attributes. Good.

Now, key issue: `emit` delete path. `w.reported[snapshot.Index]` attributes used as delete event attributes. But `attributes = previous.attributes` — this map is the stored clone. It's sent to the channel. Note `track` stores `maps.Clone(attributes)`, so the stored map is a private copy. OK.

Actually it computes attributes unconditionally at line 682 before checking reported. When previous exists, the freshly computed attributes are discarded. `interfaceAttributes` calls `overlayToolsInterface` which calls `clablinks.ToolsInterface` → `netlink.LinkByName` — a netlink syscall in root namespace. For every delete event of a tracked link this is a wasted syscall. Minor performance, P4-ish. Actually wait, when previous exists, the computed attributes are unused — dead computation. Small but could be reordered. That's a minor perf/cleanliness point.

More important potential issues:

A. `reportStitchMarks` is only called on netemTicker ticks, and the netem poll interval defaults to 1s. But it scans all states and for each candidate veth reported recently, calls `interfaceAttributes` → `ToolsInterface` → `netlink.LinkByName`. If the stitch hasn't been marked yet, this returns not-ok, so each tick it retries. That's a netlink syscall per candidate link per tick for up to 1 minute (stitchMarkWindow). That's a deliberate design ("how long after a link is first reported its host-side stitch interface is looked for"). Cost: per-tick per-link LinkByName for up to a minute. Bounded by stitchMarkWindow. Probably acceptable; ponytail ceiling maybe. Could note as P4.

<path>/source/head/docs/cmd/events.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # events command
2: 
3: ## Description
4: 
5: The `events` command streams lifecycle updates for every Containerlab resource and augments them with interface change notifications collected from the container network namespaces. The output combines the selected runtime's event feed (for example Docker) with the netlink information that powers `containerlab inspect interfaces`, so you can observe container activity and interface state changes in real time without selecting a specific lab.
6: 
7: ## Usage
8: 
9: `containerlab [global-flags] events [local-flags]`
10: 
11: **aliases:** `ev`
12: 
13: The command respects the global flags such as `--runtime`, `--debug`, or `--log-level`. It adds local options:
14: 
15: - `--format` controls the output representation (`plain`, `json`).
16: - `--initial-state` emits a snapshot of currently running containers and their interface states before following live updates.
17: - `--interface-stats` enables periodic interface counter sampling; leave unset to report only lifecycle and state changes.
18: - `--interface-stats-interval` customizes how frequently statistics are collected (for example `500ms`, `2s`, `1m`).
19: 
20: When invoked with no arguments it discovers all running labs and immediately begins streaming events; new labs that start after the command begins are picked up automatically.
21: 
22: ## Event format
23: 
24: In the default `plain` format every line mirrors the `docker events` format:
25: 
26: ```
27: <timestamp> <type> <action> <actor> (<key>=<value>, ...)
28: ```
29: 
30: - **Runtime events** show the short container ID as the actor and include the original attributes supplied by the container runtime (for example `image`, `name`, `containerlab`, `scope`, …). Container events are also enriched with `mgmt_ipv4`, `mgmt_ipv6`, and `ports` (published/exposed ports) when those values are available. When `--initial-state` is enabled the stream starts with `container <state>` snapshots (for example `container running`) that carry an `origin=snapshot` attribute.
31: - **Interface events** use type `interface` and `origin=netlink` in the attribute list. They also report interface-specific data such as `ifname`, `state`, `mtu`, `mac`, `type`, `alias`, and the lab label. The actor is still the container short ID, and the container name is supplied in the attributes (`name=...`).
32: - Interface notifications are emitted when a link appears, disappears, or when its relevant properties (operational state, MTU, alias, MAC address, type) change. Initial snapshots use the `snapshot` action when `--initial-state` is requested. When interface statistics are enabled the stream also includes `interface stats` updates with byte/packet counters and rate estimates.
33: 
34: When `--format json` is used, each event becomes a single JSON object on its own line. The fields match the plain output (`timestamp`, `type`, `action`, `actor_id`, `actor_name`, `actor_full_id`) and include an `attributes` map with the same key/value pairs that the plain formatter prints.
35: 
36: ## Examples
37: 
38: ### Watch an existing lab and new deployments
39: 
40: ```
41: $ containerlab events
42: 2024-07-01T11:02:56.123456000Z container start 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (containerlab=frr-lab, image=ghcr.io/srl-labs/frr, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, ports=0.0.0.0:2222/tcp->22;0.0.0.0:830/tcp->830)
43: 2024-07-01T11:02:57.004321000Z interface create 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (ifname=eth0, index=22, lab=frr-lab, mac=02:42:ac:14:00:02, mtu=1500, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, origin=netlink, state=up, type=veth)
44: 2024-07-01T11:02:57.104512000Z interface update 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (ifname=eth0, index=22, lab=frr-lab, mac=02:42:ac:14:00:02, mtu=9000, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, origin=netlink, state=up, type=veth)
45: 2024-07-01T11:05:12.918273000Z container die 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (containerlab=frr-lab, exitCode=0, image=ghcr.io/srl-labs/frr, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01)
46: 2024-07-01T11:05:13.018456000Z interface delete 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (ifname=eth0, index=22, lab=frr-lab, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, origin=netlink, state=up, type=veth)
47: ```
48: 
49: The stream contains all currently running labs and stays active to capture subsequent deployments, restarts, or interface adjustments.
50: 
51: ### Include existing resources in the stream
52: 
53: ```
54: $ containerlab events --initial-state
55: 2024-07-01T11:02:55.912345000Z container running 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (containerlab=frr-lab, image=ghcr.io/srl-labs/frr, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, origin=snapshot, ports=0.0.0.0:2222/tcp->22;0.0.0.0:830/tcp->830, state=running)
56: 2024-07-01T11:02:55.912678000Z interface snapshot 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (ifname=eth0, index=22, lab=frr-lab, mac=02:42:ac:14:00:02, mtu=1500, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, origin=netlink, state=up, type=veth)
57: …
58: ```
59: 
60: This mode begins with a point-in-time view of every running container and interface before switching to live updates.
61: 
62: ### Include interface statistics
63: 
64: ```
65: containerlab events --interface-stats
66: ```
67: 
68: Statistics are disabled by default. Enabling them augments the feed with periodic counter samples in addition to lifecycle and state changes. Use `--interface-stats-interval` to balance fidelity with overhead: values between `1s` and `5s` work well for most labs, while larger deployments may prefer longer intervals (for example `10s`) to avoid excessive sampling load.
69: 
70: ### Use with alternative runtimes
71: 
72: Containerlab streams events from the runtime selected via the global `--runtime` flag.
73: 
74: Currently supported runtimes:
75: 
76: - `docker`
77: - `c9s`
78: 
79: With `--runtime c9s`, events are backed by kubernetes watches for Clabernetes
80: `Topology` and `Node` resources and labeled Pods. They use the actions and
81: attributes of the Docker runtime: a node is a container (`create`, `start`,
82: `kill`, `stop`, `die`, `destroy`, `health_status: …`) described by the same
83: labels, and the interface events (`snapshot`, `create`, `update`, `delete`,
84: `stats`) report the node's lab link interfaces, `eth0` and `lo` under the names
85: a Docker container of the node has. Interface state and statistics are sampled
86: inside the device containers, not through local host netlink. The stream covers
87: every namespace by default; `--namespace` (or `CLAB_KUBE_NAMESPACE`) limits it
88: to one namespace, and `-t`/`--name` to one lab. With Docker and Podman the lab
89: selection is ignored and every lab is streamed. The
90: [c9s runtime manual](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#events) lists the
91: attributes and the few Docker attributes c9s has no equivalent for.
92: 
93: Runtimes that do not implement the `events` API, or are not yet supported by Containerlab, will exit with an explanatory error.
94: 
95: ## See also
96: 
97: - [`inspect interfaces`](inspect/interfaces.md) – produces a point-in-time view of the same interface details that `events` reports continuously.
98: - [Clabernetes containerlab runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#events) - describes c9s events and how they map onto Docker events.
99: - `docker events` – the raw runtime feed that Containerlab builds upon.

(End of file - total 99 lines)
</content>

origin=snapshot, ports=0.0.0.0:2222/tcp->22;0.0.0.0:830/tcp->830, state=running)\n2024-07-01T11:02:55.912678000Z interface snapshot 5d0b5a9ad3f1 (ifname=eth0, index=22, lab=frr-lab, mac=02:42:ac:14:00:02, mtu=1500, name=clab-frr-lab-frr01, origin=netlink, state=up, type=veth)\n…\n```\n\nThis mode begins with a point-in-time view of every running container and interface before switching to live updates.\n\n### Include interface statistics\n\n```\ncontainerlab events --interface-stats\n```\n\nStatistics are disabled by default. Enabling them augments the feed with periodic counter samples in addition to lifecycle and state changes. Use `--interface-stats-interval` to balance fidelity with overhead: values between `1s` and `5s` work well for most labs, while larger deployments may prefer longer intervals (for example `10s`) to avoid excessive sampling load.\n\n### Use with alternative runtimes\n\nContainerlab streams events from the runtime selected via the global `--runtime` flag.\n\nCurrently supported runtimes:\n\n- `docker`\n- `c9s`\n\nWith `--runtime c9s`, events are backed by kubernetes watches for Clabernetes\n`Topology` and `Node` resources and labeled Pods. They use the actions and\nattributes of the Docker runtime: a node is a container (`create`, `start`,\n`kill`, `stop`, `die`, `destroy`, `health_status: …`) described by the same\nlabels, and the interface events (`snapshot`, `create`, `update`, `delete`,\n`stats`) report the node's lab link interfaces, `eth0` and `lo` under the names\na Docker container of the node has. Interface state and statistics are sampled\ninside the device containers, not through local host netlink. The stream covers\nevery namespace by default; `--namespace` (or `CLAB_KUBE_NAMESPACE`) limits it\nto one namespace, and `-t`/`--name` to one lab. With Docker and Podman the lab\nselection is ignored and every lab is streamed. The\n[c9s runtime manual](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#events) lists the\nattributes and the few Docker attributes c9s has no equivalent for.\n\nRuntimes that do not implement the `events` API, or are not yet supported by Containerlab, will exit with an explanatory error.\n\n## See also\n\n- [`inspect interfaces`](inspect/interfaces.md) – produces a point-in-time view of the same interface details that `events` reports continuously.\n- [Clabernetes containerlab runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#events) - describes c9s events and how they map onto Docker events.\n- `docker events` – the raw runtime feed that Containerlab builds upon.",

step-start
{
  "type": "step-start",
  "id": "prt_1d46ddce2001h64LgQMy81GERg",
  "sessionID": "ses_e2b95e719ffeoD7jkbBhYGJfgm",
  "messageID": "msg_1d46dd0850019pu9eWPwYXBzD3"

Reasoning
Docs: The events doc says interface notifications are emitted when a link "appears, disappears, or when its relevant properties change". The new behavior adds: an interface can be reported under the host-side stitch interface name (ifname=clab-s-xxx) — previously, `overlayToolsInterface` already rewrote ifname for existing events, so events already reported stitch names when the stitch interface existed. The new behavior adds: on rename, delete(old)+create(new) instead of a plain update. And the new polling path means a link may appear first under the node name and then be re-reported as delete+create under the stitch name. Docs don't mention this; but they also never documented the ifname rewrite. This is a user-visible behavior change (event consumers e.g. VS Code extension may see delete+create for the same logical interface). Worth a P3/P4 doc note? The events doc line 32 describes when notifications are emitted. The new delete+create sequence for the same underlying link is a behavior consumers may need to handle. I'd flag a P4 documentation gap: docs/cmd/events.md (and possibly release notes) not updated to mention that an interface reported under its node-side name can be re-reported as delete+create when the host-side stitch interface is marked.

Now check for correctness issues more carefully.

Issue candidate 1: `emit` delete path computes `interfaceAttributes` before checking `w.reported` — when previous exists, computed attributes discarded. interfaceAttributes → overlayToolsInterface → clablinks.ToolsInterface → netlink.LinkByName (root netns syscall). So every delete of a tracked link does a wasted netlink lookup. Also for non-stitched labs, TopoIdentity returns empty → early return, cheap. For stitched labs, an extra syscall per delete event. Minor; P4.

Wait actually, is it wasted? `attributes := interfaceAttributes(...)` then `if previous, ok := ...; ok { attributes = previous.attributes }`. Yes, discarded. Could reorder: check reported first, only compute attributes if not known. P4 perf.

Issue candidate 2: `reportStitchMarks` scan cost: for each tick (default 1s), iterates all states; for each veth reported under own name within window, calls interfaceAttributes → LinkByName. Bounded by window (1 min) and number of veths. This is the designed mechanism. Fine — deliberate ceiling; the comment explains it. No ponytail: marker but there's a rationale comment. Per ponytail rule, "Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling ... with a ponytail: comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path." The window is a deliberate ceiling. Hmm, the comment does name the ceiling ("how long after a link is first reported its host-side stitch interface is looked for"). I think that satisfies the spirit. I won't flag.

Issue candidate 3: The `since` never-refresh problem: `since` = first report time of that index; if the lab takes >1 minute between creating the node-side veth and marking the stitch, the rename is missed and the interface remains reported under the node name indefinitely (until the link itself changes or resubscribe). Also `since` is preserved across resubscribes and across link updates — actually since is preserved when known. So e.g. a big Clos lab where a node's interface is created at t=0, marked at t=90s → never reported with stitch name. Then when the node's link later changes (e.g. state up), emit → track → rename detected (if stitch now exists) → delete+create. So the missed window is only permanent if the link never changes again and no resubscribe. For a veth that just sits there, that's plausible: interface reported as `eth1` instead of `clab-s-xxxx` forever.

Hmm wait, actually also consider: `reportStitchMarks` runs on netem ticker ticks — the ticker exists regardless of stats being enabled. Default 1s. OK.

But there's another subtle problem with the window: `since` is when the link was FIRST reported — for links that existed before the watcher started (includeSnapshot=true, snapshot events), since = subscription start. For long-running labs redeploying one link: the existing link was snapshotted at watcher start (t0). Redeploy of that link later: link deleted & recreated? Actually redeploy destroys the container → watcher stopped, new watcher. Hmm.

Scenario: container started at t0, watcher reports snapshot. At t0+5min, user creates a veth-stitch link attached to this container's existing interface? Not how it works — links are created during deploy before the node starts, so the stitch mark happens during deploy, right after container start typically. So the 1-minute window covers normal deploy. Slow deploys (large labs, slow image pulls) could exceed it. Is the mark done after container start? Deploy creates veth segments: node end in node netns — requires the netns to exist, i.e., container started. So the mark happens around container start; watcher starts on container start event. Time between watcher seeing the link and the mark is small (same deploy loop). So the window is mostly fine; but if the watcher subscribes late (ns path retries ~1s each, up to...), or system load... Edge case. Also note: the reported map entry survives from the FIRST report — for a link present before the watcher started (restarted container? No—new watcher). Actually when a container restarts, the watcher is stopped and restarted fresh, so reported is fresh; the links exist at subscription → snapshot events → since = subscription time. Stitch marks already exist at that point (from the original deploy) → reportStitchMarks: reported.attributes["ifname"] — wait, at snapshot emit, interfaceAttributes resolves the stitch (it exists already) → ifname = stitch name ≠ snapshot.Name → reportStitchMarks skips. Good.

Hmm wait, one more: does `reportStitchMarks` need to run even when the rename already happened? No.

Issue candidate 4: In `reportStitchMarks`, the emitted update triggers track which sends "delete" of old name. But what if the old name was never actually reported to clients? The `reported` map is populated by `track` on every emit — including events sent to the channel. So previous.attributes was actually sent. OK consistent.

Issue candidate 5: `w.reported` nil-map handling: `track` lazily creates the map. `reportStitchMarks` reads `w.reported[idx]` on a nil map — reading a nil map is fine (returns zero). OK.

Issue candidate 6: Concurrency — all calls on w.reported happen from the watcher goroutine (run → watch). Verified: emit, emitStats, reportStitchMarks, track all called from within watch/collectAndEmitStats which run in the watcher goroutine. Tests call them directly. No race. Good.

Issue candidate 7: `emitStats` when track returns true (rename) sends "create" with metrics.Timestamp then "stats". But it does NOT send a "delete" of the old name? It does — inside track. OK.

But notice: in emitStats, the create event uses `maps.Clone(attributes)` and the stats event mutates and sends `attributes`. But the stored `w.reported` clone (from track) — the stats event's attributes map is a different map (the one from interfaceAttributes) than the stored clone. Fine.

Issue candidate 8: Ordering anomaly in `watch`: on the netemTicker tick, `pollNetemChanges` then `reportStitchMarks`. Fine.

Issue candidate 9: `reportStitchMarks` condition uses `interfaceAttributes(w.container, snapshot)["ifname"] != snapshot.Name`. But `interfaceAttributes` includes netem attrs etc. — fine. One subtlety: `overlayToolsInterface` sets ifname from stitch link snapshot.Name. If the stitch link's actual name equals... the stitch far-end name is "clab-s-XXXXXXXX" (the host veth name) while the altname is "clab-stitch-...". `ToolsInterface` looks up by altname `StitchAltName(...)`, then `snapshotFromLink(tools)` — attrs.Name of a link looked up by altname: netlink.LinkByName with an altname returns the link; what is attrs.Name? The link's primary name ("clab-s-...")? Hmm — actually netlink returns the link with its primary ifname; altnames come in the altname list attribute. `netlink.LinkByName` does handle altnames (kernel supports IFLA_ALT_IFNAME lookup; the vishvananda lib sends the name and kernel matches altname too). The returned link's Attrs().Name would be the primary name "clab-s-0011aabb". Wait but does the lib parse altnames into Attrs().AltNames? Newer versions do (ILFA_PROP_LIST). Anyway, attrs.Name = primary name. The test uses `"ifname": "clab-s-0011aabb"` consistent with primary name. So events report ifname=clab-s-0011aabb. And `overlayToolsInterface` sets attributes["alias"] = topoName (the node's iface name/alias). So the event carries alias=eth1, ifname=clab-s-0011aabb. Consistent with pre-existing behavior (`inspect interfaces` matching). OK.

Issue candidate 10: Missing handling — `processExisting` on resubscribe when a link's stitch got marked while unsubscribed: snapshot.equal(previous) → no emit; and `states` unchanged. `reportStitchMarks` will pick it up only if within the window (since first report). If subscription was down long, missed. Same ceiling as candidate 3. Combine into one finding: the 1-minute window measured from first report can miss stitch marks in slow deploys or after subscription outages; suggest refreshing `since` per report or noting the ceiling. Severity: P3? It's a real correctness gap in the heuristic for large labs (deploy of a 50-node lab can exceed 1 minute easily — but the window is per-interface measured from when the watcher first reported THAT interface, which is around the container start, and the mark happens shortly after container start within the same deploy). Hmm, when is the node-end veth created vs when is the mark applied? Deploy creates the veth segment (node end + host far end) — the host far end is created at the same time (a veth has both ends created together!). Wait: buildVEthStitchSegment creates a LinkVEth with nodeEp and farEp (host endpoint). So the veth pair is created in one operation: node end in node netns, far end in root ns. Then markFarEnd adds the altname to the far end. In Deploy: seg.Deploy creates the veth; PostDeploy/stitchSegments marks. Between link creation and altname marking: segA.Deploy, segB.Deploy, then markFarEnd steps. So the gap is milliseconds to seconds. The watcher sees the node-end RTM_NEWLINK (create event) and within ~1s the netem ticker runs reportStitchMarks → finds the mark. So normal flow is fast.

When could it exceed 1 min? If the node's interface is created but the stitch's mark is delayed >1min — e.g., deploy of two endpoints in different containers where segB's container is slow to start: segA.Deploy (creates nodeA's veth + far end) at t0; watcher for nodeA reports it; segB container starts at t0+120s; then stitchSegments → markFarEnd at t0+120s → the altname added >1min after nodeA's link was first reported → missed! Then nodeA's interface remains reported as `eth1` instead of `clab-s-xxx` permanently (until the link changes). This is a realistic scenario: large labs where one node takes a long time to pull/start while the other endpoint is already up. Also note `PostDeploy` is where stitching happens when not both deployed in Deploy — i.e., the second node's deploy triggers the stitch. So the window measured from the FIRST endpoint's link creation covers the whole time until the second node is deployed. Slow pulls can exceed a minute easily.

Hmm, but wait — when the second node deploys and stitches, does nodeA's interface see any netlink change? markFarEnd only adds an altname to the HOST far end (in root ns), not in nodeA's netns. `stitch` — let me check what stitch does (tc mirror/redirect). It applies tc rules on host-side interfaces. So no nodeA-netns event. So indeed the rename event for nodeA would be missed if >1min gap. And then the reported name stays "eth1" — the client (VS Code extension / events consumer) sees the wrong interface name for that link. Impact: incorrect/stale reporting for veth-stitch links in slow deploys; self-corrects only on later node-side link change or resubscribe. That's a genuine P3 (correctness/reliability, bounded impact, heuristic by design).

Also the `since` is "when the link was first reported" — one could refresh `since` on every track (each emit/update), which would widen coverage: after the second endpoint deploys, are there updates on nodeA's interface? Probably not. So refreshing wouldn't help in that scenario either; the real fix would be a longer window or event-based detection (netlink monitor in root ns for altname addition isn't possible — altname changes do generate RTM_NEWLINK notifications in the root namespace! Actually LinkAddAltName triggers a NEWLINK notification in root ns. But the watcher subscribes in the node's netns, not root. Could the registry watch root-ns altname additions? That'd be more code.) The simplest correction: make the window more generous or document the ceiling with a `ponytail:` comment. I'll flag P3 with concrete trigger.

Actually, let me reconsider severity: the impact is an events-stream consumer seeing the node-side name for a stitched link (the pre-PR behavior, actually — before this PR, the interface was ALWAYS reported under the node name unless the link changed after marking). So the PR improves things; the window is a residual gap. P3/P4. I'd say P4 optional improvement? It's a correctness limitation of a new heuristic — but the PR is strictly improving reporting. The residual gap: slow deploys. I'd rate P4 (optional improvement, name the ceiling per ponytail). Hmm, but the rule says mark with a ponytail comment. The finding could be: "Stitch-mark window can miss renames in slow deploys; add ponytail ceiling comment or refresh." P4.

Issue candidate 11: Test `TestReportStitchMarksLeavesUnmarkedLinks` — "reported too long ago, are not reported again" — good. But no positive test for reportStitchMarks (a link whose stitch exists gets delete+create). The emit rename path is tested. Gap: reportStitchMarks's positive path (the new polling logic) is untested. Also `emitStats` non-rename path (normal stats emission) — was previously covered by other tests? Base had no direct stats test either it seems. Coverage note.

Issue candidate 12: `TestEmitStatsReportsRenamedLinkFirst` mutates `watcher.reported[7].attributes` check — fine.

Issue candidate 13: Docs: `docs/cmd/events.md` line 32 says "Interface notifications are emitted when a link appears, disappears, or when its relevant properties ... change." The new behavior: a link can be re-reported (delete+create) without any property of the node-side link changing — when its host-side stitch interface gets marked. Docs don't mention this. Also the `ifname` may change from the node name to the stitch name mid-stream. P4 doc gap. Actually is it user-visible behavior introduced by this PR? Yes — previously (base), an interface was only reported with the stitch ifname when the link itself changed after marking (overlayToolsInterface applied on each emit). The PR adds proactive rename reporting (delete+create). Consumers following the doc's model (create/update/delete keyed by ifname) will see delete+create pairs for the same interface. A doc note would help. P4.

Issue candidate 14: The delete-then-create naming: when track detects rename, it sends `delete` with `previous.attributes` — which includes the OLD attributes fully (ifname=old, alias, index, mtu...). Good.

Issue candidate 15: Now — a potential real bug: in `emit`, for action "update" where track returns false (same name), the event is sent as "update". But consider the case where the link was reported under the same ifname but the STITCH now exists with different attributes... can't happen: if stitch exists, ifname differs from node name → rename path.

Consider the reverse: stitch existed (reported as clab-s-x), then the stitch altname is REMOVED (link destroyed) — next emit (e.g., node-side update) resolves ifname back to node name → rename detected → delete(clab-s-x) + create(eth1). Good, handled.

But: when the whole veth-stitch link is destroyed, both node end and host far end are deleted. Node-ns watcher sees RTM_DELLINK → processUpdate: exists → emit delete with previous.attributes (stitch name). Good — matches TestEmitDeletesLinkAsLastReported.

Issue candidate 16: `reportStitchMarks` — what if `w.container` is nil? Guarded in emit; interfaceAttributes(w.container, snapshot) with nil container → containerLabel(nil) → "", firstContainerName(nil) → "", overlayToolsInterface(nil) → return early. So attributes["ifname"] == snapshot.Name → no emit. Safe.

Issue candidate 17: `interfaceAttributes` is called twice for the same snapshot in reportStitchMarks → emit path: reportStitchMarks calls interfaceAttributes (1), then emit calls interfaceAttributes again (2). Two LinkByName lookups per marked link. Minor.

Issue candidate 18: A real correctness question about `track` + `emit` action semantics: `processExisting` on resubscribe emits "update" for changed links and "create" for new. If a link reported before resubscribe under stitch name, and now reported under node name (e.g., stitch altname removed while down) → rename → delete+create. fine.

What about `processExisting` first subscription with includeSnapshot=true: emits "snapshot". track populates reported. If the snapshot event resolves to stitch name, reported = stitch attrs. fine.

Issue candidate 19: In `emit`, when action is "create" and track detects rename (returns true), action stays "create" — so sequence delete(old), create(new). Good.

When action is "snapshot" and track returns true — impossible as discussed (fresh watcher). Except... hmm, is `emit` ever called with "snapshot" when reported is non-nil? `initial` is only true on the first watch call; reported is empty then. OK.

Issue candidate 20: A subtle one — `emit`'s delete path when `previous` does NOT exist: attributes freshly computed (with stitch if stitched). Fine.

Issue candidate 21: `interfaceAttributes` in the delete path of emit: if previous exists, the computed attributes are discarded — but wait, there's a cost concern already noted. Also in the delete path, if the link was never reported but the stitch exists, delete event reports ifname = stitch name — client never saw it. Pre-existing-ish (base: delete for a state-tracked but never-reported link — with stitch, ifname rewritten). OK.

Issue candidate 22: Now check `TestEmitReportsRenamedLinkDeleted` expectations: first emit "update": track → previous ifname "clab-s-0011aabb" ≠ "e1-1" → send delete(clab-s-0011aabb), store new, return true → action "update"→"create" → send create(e1-1). Second emit "update": track → same name → return false → send update(e1-1). Want: [delete clab-s, create e1-1, update e1-1]. Matches.

But note: `registry.sendInterfaceEvent(w.container, "delete", previous.attributes, time.Now())` inside track — `previous.attributes` is the stored clone. Sent to channel. Consumers get a reference to... the map is shared with w.reported until deleted — the map is only referenced by w.reported[index].attributes and now the event. After track replaces w.reported[index] with a NEW clone, no aliasing. OK.

Issue candidate 23: Now — check the events flow consumer side: aggregatedEvent attributes map is passed to consumers. Since we send stored clones and interfaceAttributes-fresh maps, no shared mutable state issues.

Issue candidate 24: What about `w.reported` growing unboundedly? Entries are deleted on emit("delete"). Links deleted while subscription down: removeVanishedLinks → emit delete → cleaned. But: a link that was in `states` but never reported (includeSnapshot=false first subscription) then deleted → removeVanishedLinks emits delete with fresh attributes — reported has no entry → nothing to delete; fine. And processUpdate RTM_DELLINK requires exists in states. States and reported can drift: a link in states but not reported (snapshot-suppressed). When that link is later updated → emit "update" → track adds to reported. So reported ⊆ states roughly, and delete paths clean both. Bounded by number of interfaces. Fine.

Hmm wait, actually there's an asymmetry bug candidate: In `removeVanishedLinks` and `processUpdate` RTM_DELLINK, states entry deleted, emit(delete) → reported entry deleted. But what about the reverse: reported entry exists without states entry? Could happen? track is only called via emit which is called with snapshots that are in states (processExisting/processUpdate/pollNetemChanges all set states before/around emit) — except `reportStitchMarks` iterates states. emitStats is called for state in states. So reported tracks states. When states entry deleted, emit delete cleans reported. Consistent.

Hmm — one more: `processUpdate` RTM_NEWLINK for a link not in states but... emits create → track adds. Fine.

Issue candidate 25: Check `emitStats` guard order: `!registry.includeStats` — registry.includeStats is set from the registry's global includeStats; watcher also has includeStats. Pre-existing pattern (emitInterfaceStatsEvent used r.includeStats). Same.

Issue candidate 26: `run()` resubscribe: `states` persists; `reported` persists. Good.

Issue candidate 27: The netem ticker runs `reportStitchMarks` every second even when there are no veth links — iterating states map each tick: O(#interfaces) per second per container, cheap (map iteration). For a container with many interfaces (say 100), 100 map reads/sec — trivial. But `interfaceAttributes` (with LinkByName syscall) only for candidates. OK.

Issue candidate 28: One more possible issue: `reportStitchMarks` calls `interfaceAttributes` which includes `overlayToolsInterface` — this does a `netlink.LinkByName` in the ROOT namespace. But the watcher goroutine — does it need to be in any particular netns for that call? `netlink.LinkByName` without a handle uses the current thread's netns. The watcher... does `watch` change the thread's netns? It uses `netlink.NewHandleAt(nsHandle)` — handle-based calls specify the netns. But `ToolsInterface` calls the package-level `netlink.LinkByName`, which operates on the current process/thread netns (root, since containerlab CLI runs in root ns). Does anything put the watcher goroutine's thread into the container netns? `netns.GetFromPath` just opens an fd. `netlink.LinkSubscribeWithOptions` with Namespace option — internally it does `netns.Set`? I recall it does `netns.Set(ns)` on a locked OS thread within the internal socket goroutine, not the caller. Hmm, actually in netlink lib, `LinkSubscribeWithOptions` → `LinkSubscribeAt` with `EnterNetNS`? There's a `socketInNetns` helper: if Namespace is set, it locks the OS thread and sets the netns to create the socket, then restores. The socket goroutine is internal. The caller's thread netns unchanged.

<content>
140: A reboot issued from within an [SR Linux](../manual/kinds/srl.md#reboot) node, such as `tools platform chassis reboot`, now brings the node back with its links, saved configuration and management address intact.
141: 
142: Each `nokia_srlinux` node uses an internal `<node-name>-netns` holder container for its network namespace. The holder is hidden from `inspect` unless `--all` is set and is removed with the node. SR Linux nodes now default to the `unless-stopped` restart policy, while an explicitly configured policy is preserved. Nodes with a user-defined `network-mode` keep the previous behavior.
143: 
144: Done in #3567 thanks to @sacckth and @kaelemc
145: 
146: ## Per-node runtime deprecated
147: 
148: The node-level `runtime` parameter is deprecated and will be removed in a future release. Mixing runtimes within a lab is not supported because management networking and lab-wide operations use the lab-wide runtime. Setting `runtime` on a node, group, kind or defaults block still works but emits a deprecation notice.
149: 
150: Use the global `--runtime` flag or `CLAB_RUNTIME` environment variable instead:
151: 
152: ```bash
153: containerlab deploy -t mylab.clab.yml --runtime podman
154: ```
155: 
156: Done in #3527 thanks to @FloSch62
157: 
158: ## Miscellaneous
159: 
160: - `tools snapshot save` now snapshots the deployed nodes of a partially built lab instead of failing on nodes whose containers were not created. [#3537](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3537)
161: - Ciena SAOS nodes now stage a topology-supplied license file—provided as a path, URL or embedded blob—into `/config/license` and expose it to the vrnetlab launcher through `SAOS_LICENSE_PATH`. [#3535](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3535)
162: - SR-SIM finds its running instance using the full process command line, fixing discovery when FEX emulates SR OS on ARM hosts. [#3538](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3538)
163: - `containerlab events` no longer misses interfaces created while a watcher starts. Failed netlink subscriptions are retried and re-established, with changes during the outage reconciled instead of silently ending interface reporting. [#3532](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3532)
164: - FRR keeps `vtysh` available throughout installation of the configuration-save wrapper, closing a startup race that could prevent the initial configuration from loading. [#3555](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3555)
165: - Cisco IOL now propagates a `mgmt-intf` override to the VM launcher when a non-default management interface is selected. [#3557](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3557)
166: - Destroy and reconfigure operations reliably remove `vxlan-stitch` host interfaces by waiting for node deletion before host-link cleanup, including partial labs with no remaining containers. [#3571](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3571)
167: - Open vSwitch bridges no longer accumulate stale auto-generated `clab-*` ports across deploy and redeploy operations when endpoint names require sanitized Linux interface names. Destroy also cleans up orphaned generated ports, and `ovs-vsctl` cleanup calls time out instead of hanging when `ovs-vswitchd` is unavailable. [#3573](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3573)
168: - Empty node, kind and defaults definitions are handled correctly: nodes without a body inherit kind, group and configuration values, while empty kind or defaults blocks no longer cause panics. [#3518](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3518)
169: - Docker and Podman resolve `network-mode: container:` targets correctly when a lab name contains the node name, fixing deployments of SR Linux nodes with their new network-namespace holders. [#3577](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3577)
170: - The go-jose dependency was updated to 4.1.8. [#3569](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3569)
171: - Documentation was clarified for [OcNOS image requirements](../manual/kinds/ipinfusion-ocnos.md), HwSKU-dependent [Dell SONiC interface names](../manual/kinds/dell_sonic.md), and [SR Linux platform licensing](../manual/kinds/srl.md#license). [#3552](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3552) [#3553](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3553) [#3575](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/pull/3575)

(End of file - total 171 lines)

## Objective
- Review a read-only PR snapshot at `/source` (repo: srl-labs/containerlab, head `1c9b3577d08f7524fd228e5ab4df3dd3fc107f53`, base `f57133d88d7f26767e78a9f4c0f81a9ed070fe8b`) against standards.json rules (karpathy-guidelines.mdc, ponytail.mdc), tracking both changed paths and relevant callers/tests/docs/schema, then return a trusted result schema with findings.

## Important Details
- Changed paths (manifest.json): `core/events/netlink.go` (M) and `core/events/netlink_watch_test.go` (M). Both are modified.
- Snapshot layout: `/source/head/`, `/source/base/`, `/source/diff.txt`, `/source/manifest.json`, `/source/standards.json`, `/source/standards/*.mdc`.
- Note: direct `read` on `/source` (directory) is denied by permission rules; must read concrete file paths.
- PR summary (from diff analysis):
  - `netlinkWatcher` gains a `reported map[int]reportedInterface` (per-interface last-reported attributes + `since` time).
  - New `reportedInterface{attributes map[string]string; since time.Time}` type.
  - New package-level `var stitchMarkWindow = time.Minute` — window during which host-side stitch interfaces are polled for after a link is first reported.
  - New watcher methods: `emit` (tracks reported state; converts `update`→`create` after emitting a `delete` under the old ifname when rename detected), `emitStats` (emits create-without-counters on rename before stats event), `track` (delete old name + store `maps.Clone(attributes)`), `reportStitchMarks` (called on netem ticker tick; for veth links reported under their own name within `stitchMarkWindow`, resolves host-side stitch via `interfaceAttributes` → `overlayToolsInterface` → `clablinks.ToolsInterface` → `netlink.LinkByName` in root netns; emits update when stitch altname exists).
  - `sendInterfaceEvent` (registry method) replaced base `emitInterfaceEvent` and lost the base's `container == nil` guard — callers verified as guarded (`emit`/`emitStats` check `w.container == nil`; `track` only reached via those).
  - `w.reported` is only touched from the watch-loop goroutine (no race); persists across resubscribes.
- Analysis findings so far (candidate findings, not yet finalized):
  - P4 candidate: wasted `interfaceAttributes` call (root-netns `LinkByName` syscall) in `emit`'s delete path when `previous` exists — result discarded.
  - P4 candidate: `since` never refreshes (window measured from FIRST report); slow lab deploys (>1 min between node link creation and stitch mark) miss the rename, though it self-corrects on any later link update or resubscribe. No `ponytail:` comment marking the deliberate ceiling (ponytail rule requires naming ceiling/upgrade path).
  - Pre-existing (not worsened): with `includeSnapshot=false`, links present at first subscription are in `states` but not `w.reported`; later update/delete events reference never-reported interfaces. Same in base.
  - Test coverage gap: `reportStitchMarks` rename path (positive case) is untested; only negative case tested (`TestReportStitchMarksLeavesUnmarkedLinks`).
- Constraints: source-only review, no test execution; must disclose that limitation. Use read offsets for truncated output, group independent tool calls, glob/grep for surrounding code. Must return the trusted result schema at the end.

673: // reported. A link reported under another name before, such as the node's own

# Review: stitched-interface rename reporting in the netlink watcher

## Summary

This PR improves how `clab events` reports veth-stitch links: a per-interface record of the last-reported attributes (`w.reported`) lets the watcher detect that a link is now known under its host-side stitch interface name, emit a `delete` under the old name, and re-`create` it under the stitch name — both on live link updates and proactively, via a one-second netem-ticker poll (`reportStitchMarks`) within a one-minute window after the link was first reported. The overall design is lean, the rename handling is consistent across `emit`, `emitStats`, and `track`, and the tests cover the rename mechanics well. I found no correctness defects; the findings below are minor efficiency, documentation, and test-coverage improvements.

| Priority | Count | Summary |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | 0 | — |
| P1 | 0 | — |
| P2 | 0 | — |
| P3 | 0 | — |
| P4 | 4 | Wasted root-netns lookup on delete; undocumented event-stream behavior change; stitch-window ceiling unmarked/untested positive path |

## Findings

### 1. `emit` computes attributes before consulting `w.reported`, wasting a root-netns lookup on every delete (P4)

**Location:** [core/events/netlink.go:681-687](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink.go#L681-L687) (head)

**Problem:** In the `delete` branch, `attributes := interfaceAttributes(w.container, snapshot)` is computed first, and only then overwritten with `previous.attributes` when the index is known. `interfaceAttributes` ends in `overlayToolsInterface` → `clablinks.ToolsInterface` → `netlink.LinkByName`, a root-netns `RTM_GETLINK` round trip ([core/events/netlink.go:852-867](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink.go#L852-L867), [core/events/netlink.go:898-919](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink.go#L898-L919)).

**Trigger:** Every interface `delete` event for a previously reported interface — i.e., the common case for link teardown during `destroy` or `redeploy`.

**Impact:** One wasted netlink lookup per delete event. Small in absolute terms, but free to avoid.

**Correction:** Check `w.reported[snapshot.Index]` first and only call `interfaceAttributes` when no previous report exists:

```go
if action == "delete" {
    previous, ok := w.reported[snapshot.Index]
    attributes := previous.attributes
    if !ok {
        attributes = interfaceAttributes(w.container, snapshot)
    }
    delete(w.reported, snapshot.Index)
    ...
}
```

### 2. The stitch-mark window is measured from the first report, so slow stitching is silently missed; the ceiling is not marked per the ponytail rule (P4)

**Location:** [core/events/netlink.go:650-670](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink.go#L650-L670) and [core/events/netlink.go:736-743](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink.go#L736-L743) (head)

**Problem:** `track` preserves the original `since` timestamp (`since = previous.since`) forever, and `reportStitchMarks` skips any link where `time.Since(reported.since) > stitchMarkWindow` (one minute). The window therefore measures from the *first* time the watcher reported the link, not from the most recent poll. In a veth-stitch deploy where the opposite endpoint's container starts slowly (image pull, slow node startup), stitching happens in `PostDeploy` after the first node's interface was already reported — potentially more than a minute later. The rename is then never reported: the interface stays under the node-side name (`eth1`) until the node-side link itself changes or the watcher resubscribes. Note that refreshing `since` on every poll is not the right fix either — it would keep every veth a polling candidate forever and turn the window into an unbounded scan; the one-minute bound is a deliberate cost ceiling.

**Trigger:** Slow deploys where the gap between a node-end veth being created and its host-side altname being applied exceeds one minute; also subscription outages longer than the window.

**Impact:** For those links, events report `ifname: eth1` instead of the stitch name — the same limitation the base code had, so this is a residual gap in an improvement, not a regression. But the repository's ponytail rule asks that deliberate ceilings carry a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and the upgrade path; the comment on `reportStitchMarks` explains the mechanism but not this tradeoff.

**Correction:** Add a `ponytail:` comment at `stitchMarkWindow` stating the ceiling (e.g., "links stitched more than one minute after first report are not renamed in the stream until the link changes or the watcher resubscribes; a root-namespace monitor for altname additions would close this"). Optionally extend the window if deploy observations show it is tight.

### 3. The new delete+create re-report of stitched links is undocumented (P4)

**Location:** [docs/cmd/events.md:32](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/docs/cmd/events.md#L32) (head), introduced by [core/events/netlink.go:672-699](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink.go#L672-L699)

**Problem:** The events documentation states that interface notifications are emitted "when a link appears, disappears, or when its relevant properties … change." This PR adds a new class of emission: a link whose properties did not change can be re-reported as a `delete` under its node-side name followed by a `create` under the host-side stitch interface name, both live (via `track`) and proactively (via `reportStitchMarks`). Consumers that key records on `ifname` — the documented contract — will see teardown+creation pairs for the same logical interface whenever a stitch is marked.

**Trigger:** Any consumer of `clab events` watching a lab with veth-stitch links, during deploy or link reconfiguration.

**Impact:** The documented event model does not match the observable stream, which can surprise integrators (e.g., the VS Code extension) into treating the rename as a link removal and re-add.

**Correction:** Add one or two sentences to `docs/cmd/events.md` explaining that a stitched interface may be reported as `delete` under its node-side name and `create` under the stitch interface name when the host-side stitch is marked, and that `alias` carries the topology name throughout. Since release 0.83 is the current unreleased release, a short Miscellaneous bullet in `docs/rn/0.83.md` would also fit; the `release-note-review` rule itself is not violated because this PR touches no files under `docs/rn/`.

### 4. The positive path of `reportStitchMarks` is untested (P4)

**Location:** [core/events/netlink_watch_test.go:511](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/blob/<HEAD_SHA>/core/events/netlink_watch_test.go#L511) (head)

**Problem:** `TestReportStitchMarksLeavesUnmarkedLinks` covers only the negative filtering cases (stitch already reflected, expired window, never reported). The core new behavior — a within-window link reported under its own name whose host-side stitch gets marked, producing a `delete` of the old name and a `create` under the stitch name — has no direct test. The rename mechanics are covered indirectly through `TestEmitReportsRenamedLinkDeleted` and `TestEmitStatsReportsRenamedLinkFirst`, but the polling function's candidate filter (`snapshot.Type == "veth"`, name comparison, window check) and its interaction with `emit` are not exercised end to end.

**Trigger:** A future refactor of `reportStitchMarks` (e.g., reordering the window check or the type check) would not be caught by the suite.

**Impact:** Low, but this is the headline behavior of the PR, and the existing test harness already fakes stitch marks, so the missing case is cheap to add.

**Correction:** Add one test in `netlink_watch_test.go` modeled on `TestReportStitchMarksLeavesUnmarkedLinks`: pre-seed `watcher.reported` with a veth reported under its own name with a fresh `since`, fake a stitch mark in the harness, call `reportStitchMarks`, and assert the expected `delete` + `create` sequence in the collected events.

## Coverage notes

- This was a source-only review: I inspected the head and base snapshots, the full diff, both changed files, callers of the changed functions, the events documentation, release notes, and the repository's review rules. I did not execute the test suite, run `make format`, or exercise the code; statements about tests reflect source inspection only.
- All changed paths (`core/events/netlink.go`, `core/events/netlink_watch_test.go`) and their relevant surrounding code were reviewed. The base-vs-head comparison confirmed the old `container == nil` guard on interface events is preserved by the guards inside `emit` and `emitStats`; all call sites of the new `sendInterfaceEvent` are within these guarded paths.
- The pre-existing behavior that links present at the first subscription are recorded in `states` but not in `w.reported` (with `includeSnapshot=false`) was checked and is not worsened by this PR; it only matters for later `delete`/`update` events, which already fall back to freshly computed attributes.
- `w.reported` is accessed only from the watcher goroutine (`emit`, `emitStats`, `track`, `reportStitchMarks` are all invoked from `watch`/`collectAndEmitStats`), and `track` stores `maps.Clone(attributes)`, so the later mutation of the freshly built attributes map in `emitStats` cannot corrupt stored state. I found no race or aliasing issue.
- The ponytail ceiling-comment rule was applied to the `stitchMarkWindow` heuristic (finding 2); no other deliberate simplification in this PR appeared to warrant a ceiling marker.
